[fol. 1] 
IN UNITED STATES DISTRICT COURT 


No. 2049 


Borine Air Transport, Inc., a Corporation, Plaintiff, 
vs. 


Wiuuiam H. Epetman, State Treasurer; Harry R. WEsToN, 
State Treasurer; The City of Cheyenne, a Municipal 
Corporation; and The City of Rock Springs, a Municipal 
Corporation, Defendants 


Bitt or Comptaint—Filed Nov. 25, 1930 


Comes now the plaintiff, the Boeing Air Transport, Inc., 
and complaining of the said defendants, William H. Edel- 
[fol.2] man, State Treasurer, The City of Cheyenne and 
The City of Rock Springs, for cause of action alleges: 


1. That the plaintiff is a corporation organized and exist- 
ing under the laws of the State of Washington and duly 
authorized to do business of the kind which it is transacting 
as hereinafter alleged, in and through the State of Wyo- 
ming, and is a citizen and resident of said State of Wash- 
ington. 


2. That the defendant, William H. Edelman, is a citizen 
and resident of the State of Wyoming, and is an officer of 
the State of Wyoming, namely, the duly elected, qualified 
and acting State Treasurer st the State of Wyoming. 


3. That this action is a suit of a civil nature, and arises 
under the Constitution and laws of the United States, and 
the matter in controversy exceeds, exclusive of interest and 
costs, the sum and value of Three Thousand Dollars 
($3,000.00). 

Plaintiff further alleges and shows that this action is one 
in which said plaintiff seeks to enjoin the defendant, Wil- 
liam H. Edelman, a State officer of the State of Wyoming, 
from collecting an unconstitutional and illegal tax levied 


1—571 


ea 


ies ea 


Aiea ee 


a ae ai 5 


SERENE 


2 


and imposed by the State of Wyoming upon plaintiff in 
violation of Clause 3 of Section 8 of Article I of the Consti- 
tution of the United States, and that the value of the right 
of this plaintiff to transact interstate business and com- 
merce in and through the State of Wyoming, without being 
subjected to such unconstitutional, illegal and void tax, is 
far in excess of $3,000.00 exclusive of interest and costs. 
And plaintiff shows that unless the defendant shall be re- 
strained from the collection of such unconstitutional, illegal 
and invalid tax this plaintiff will be foreed to pay unto the 
defendant William H. Edelman such illegal and void taxes 
in an amount far in excess of $3,000.00 exclusive of interest 
and costs, in order to carry on its interstate business and 
commerce as hereinafter set forth. 


4. That this plaintiff is now and for a long time hitherto 
has been engaged in and intends to continue to carry on and 
transact interstate commerce and business, and is now and 
' for a long time hitherto has been engaged and intends to 
» continue to carry on and transact such interstate commerce 
and business in transporting passengers and express and 
United States mails by airplane for hire and profit, into, 
[fol.3] across and through the State of Wyoming from 
points outside the boundaries thereof and from points 
within the State of Wyoming to points outside the bound- 
aries thereof, and also a small amount of similar business 
from points inside the State of Wyoming to other points 
within said State, all of which is carried on intermingled 
in the same airplanes. 


%. That the fuel used in propelling the plaintiff’s air- 
planes is gasoline, and plaintiff knows of no other fuel 
which can be practically, conveniently or profitably used 
therefor; that the gasoline purchased by and delivered to 
the plaintiff and used by the plaintiff as fuel for the opera- 
~ tion and propulsion of its said airplanes in its commingled 
} intrastate business and commerce and interstate business 
and commerce cannot be apportioned between the said two 
classes of business. 


6. That the plaintiff owns, operates and maintains air- 
+ planes in interstate commerce and business as aforesaid, 
upon regularly defined routes and schedules, the motive 
power used by said airplanes, and each of them, being as 
aforesaid exclusively the use of gasoline as fuel.

7. That a part of such gasoline used by the plaintiff is 
purchased by and delivered to the plaintiff in the State of 
Wyoming; that a part thereof is purchased outside of said 
State, and that such gasoline purchased and delivered to 
the plaintiff either in or outside of said State of Wyoming 
is used and consumed by it as fuel for the propulsion of its 
said airplanes in its interstate commerce and business as 
aforesaid, and is an instrumentality of interstate commerce. 


8. Plaintiff further shows that in its said interstate com- 
merce business some passengers, mail and express are 
transported and carried in intrastate business, and in that 
connection plaintiff shows that the said two classes of busi- 
ness, interstate and intrastate, are so commingled that the 
separation of the one from the other is not reasonably prac- 
ticable and/or economical, and the plaintiffs’ interstate 
passengers, mail and express cannot be carried efficiently 
and economically in airplanes used exclusively in interstate 
commerce, nor can plaintiff’s intrastate passengers, mail 
and express be carried efficiently and/or economically in 
airplanes used exclusively in intrastate commerce, and that 
plaintiff's said interstate business is dependent in a con- 
[fol.4] siderable degree upon plaintiff’s intrastate busi- 
ness, and that plaintiff cannot profitably or economically 
use or operate separate airplanes in interstate business or 
separate airplanes in intrastate business. 


9. That by divers acts of the Legislature of the State of 
Wyoming, to-wit, Chapter 73 of the Session Laws of 
Wyoming of 1923, entitled ‘*An Act providing a license tax 
on gasoline, providing for the collection thereof, defining 
certain words or phrases, prescribing manner and methods 
of payment, fixing penalties for failure to make same, and 
providing for the disposition of said taxes’’; together with 
Chapter 89 of the Session Laws of Wyoming of 1925, en- 
titled ‘*An Act to amend and re-enact Sections 2, 3 and 5, 
Chapter 73, Session Laws of Wyoming, 1923, providing a 
license tax on gasoline and providing for the disposition of 
such license taxes, and display of price signs by dealers in 
gasoline and prescribing penalty for violation’’; together 
with Chapter 70 of the Session Laws of Wyoming of 1927, 
entitled ‘‘An Act to amend and re-enact Section- 2 and 3, 
Chapter 73, Session Laws of Wyoming, 1923, as amended 
and re-enacted by Sections 1 and 2, Chapter 89, Session

Laws of Wyoming, 1925, and to amend and re-enact See- 
tion 4 of Chapter 89, Session Laws of Wyoming, 1925, 
relating to display of price signs by dealers and providing 
a penalty for violation thereof; providing a license tax on 
gasoline and for the collection and statement of the amount 
of tax collected’’; together with Chapter 139 of the Session 
Laws of Wyoming of 1929, entitled ‘An Act to amend and 
re-enact Sections 2 and 3, Chapter 73, Session Laws of 
Wyoming, 1923, as amended by Sections 1 and 2, Chapter 
70, Session Laws of Wyoming, 1927, and to amend and re- 
enact Section 5, Chapter 73, Session Laws of Wyoming, 
1923, as amended and re-enacted by Section 3, Chapter 39, 
(89?) Session Laws of Wyoming, 1925, and to amend and 
re-enact Section 6, Chapter 73, Session Laws of Wyoming, 
1923; providing for a license tax on gasoline, and for the 
collection and statement of the amount of tax collected; 
providing for the disposition of said taxes, and fixing 
penalties for violation of any of the provisions of this 
Act’’; and Chapter 14 of the Session Laws of Wyoming 
enacted at the Special Session, 1929, entitled ‘‘An Act to 
amend and re-enact Section 3, Chapter 73, Session Laws of 
Wyoming, 1923, as amended and re-enacted by Section 2, 
[fol.5] Chapter 70, Session Laws of Wyoming, 1927, as 
amended and re-enacted by Section 2, Chapter 139, Session 
Laws of Wyoming, 1929, providing for a license tax on 
gasoline, and for the collection thereof and providing for 
statements of gasoline sold or used by wholesalers or users 
and the payment of the tax by them; and providing for the 
disposition of the tax from gasoline used on municipal air 
fields and fixing penalties for violation of any of the provi- 
sions of this Act’’; (each of which acts for convenience is 
quoted in full in an appendix to this bill of complaint), and 
by the construction and interpretation of the said acts by 
the defendant and by the State of Wyoming and its officers 
the said State of Wyoming imposed and levied a tax of 
four cents per gallon on each and every gallon of gasoline 
placed by the plaintiff, in its airplanes while within the 
State of Wyoniing, whether purchased or delivered to it in 
the State of Wyoming, or elsewhere, and on each and every 
gallon of gasoline put into the tanks of each of its airplanes 
within the State of Wyoming for consumption in propelling 
such airplanes in its commerce as aforesaid, and on each 
and every gallon of gasoline consumed by the plaintiff in 


5) 


the State of Wyoming in its aforesaid commerce, that 
under the provisions of said acts the defendant, Treasurer 
of the State of Wyoming, and his respective deputies, 
agents, servants, attorneys. workmen and other employees 
are charged with and exercise the duty of collecting and dis- 
bursing the said tax as therein provided; that under the 
said statutes the penalties and liabilities, civil and criminal, 
against the plaintiff, its officers and employees, for any 
failure in the payment of any tax demanded under said 
statute include among other things liability for double the 
amount of the tax, liability to injunction against the plain- 
tiff and its vendors, without bond, such as would prevent 
the plaintiff from obtaining any gasoline, liability to have 
appointed for the plaintiff a receiver for its property and 
its business, liability on the part of its officers and em- 
ployees to fine and imprisonment; that for a long time 
prior hereto the said defendant, State Treasurer, and his 
deputies, agents, servants, attorneys, workmen and/or 
employees have wrongfully and without authority of law 
required the said plaintiff to pay the said tax on each and 
every gallon of gasoline purchased or put into the tanks of 
its airplanes or used as aforesaid in the conduct of its said 
[fol. 6] interstate commerce and business, and that the said 
defendant, State Treasurer, and his respective deputies, 
agents, servants, attorneys, workmen and/or employees 
still require and threaten to continue to require the pay- 
ment of the said tax by the plaintiff, and will wrongfully 
and unlawfully continue to exact and collect the said tax 
from the plaintiff unless enjoined and restrained from so 
doing by order of this court; that the defendant, his 
deputies, employees and agents have already exacted from 
the plaintiff under the said statute, and the plaintiff to 
avoid on its own part and on that of its employees and of- 
ficers the great penalties, civil and criminal, denounced by 
said statutes, has paid to the defendant, William H. Edel- 
man, under proper and sufficient protest, the following 
sums for gasoline put into its airplane tanks at Cheyenne 
and used in the aforesaid commerce, to-wit: 


March 15, 1980 $424.08 
April 12, 1930 455.96 
May 10, 1930 651.68

[fol.7] 106. Plaintiff alleges that the said statutes of Wy- 
oming are unconstitutional, illegal and void in so far as 
they, or any or either of them, apply to plaintiff, for the 
reason that the same directly burdens and interferes with 
interstate commerce, and particularly for the reason that 
the said statutes as applied to plaintiff directly burden and 
interfere with the said interstate commerce and business of 
plaintiff in transporting and carrying mail, passengers 
and/or express in interstate commerce by air, and plaintiff 
alleges that the said tax is unconstitutional, illegal and void 
so far as it applies to plaintiff and its said business because 
it conflicts with, offends against and is in violation of Clause 
3 of Section 8 of Article I of the Constitution of the United 
States granting to Congress the exclusive power to reguiate 
commerce between the several states, in that the said stat- 
utes purport to impose and levy, and do impose and levy a 
tax on all gasoline purchased or consumed as fuel in Wyo- 
ming to propel airplanes flying between the said State and 


a 


SAT Se SA A AO RD 


neighboring States in interstate commerce, thus exacting the 
said tax as the price of the privilege of using an instrumen- 
tality of interstate commerce and thus evading the exclu- 
sive power of Congress to regulate commerce between the 
several States. 


11. Plaintiff further avers that the said defendant, the 
City of Cheyenne, and the said defendant, the City of Rock 
Springs, and each of them, are municipal corporations or- 
ganized and existing under the laws of the State of Wyom- 
ing and each of them is a citizen and resident of the State 
of Wyoming; that there is at each of the said municipal cor- 
porations a municipal air field, at each of which the air- 
planes used by this plaintiff land more or less regularly 
and more or less regularly are supplied with gasoline to be 
used as fuel in propelling its airplanes in interstate com- 
merce as aforesaid. 

Plaintiff alleges that the said statutes as construed by the 
defendants, and each of them, appropriate to the said de- 
fendant, the City of Cheyenne, all funds received from the 
said gasoline license tax on gasoline taken into the airplanes 
of the plaintiff at Cheyenne for use by the plaintiff in such 
interstate commerce and directs such fund to be paid over 
to the said City of Cheyenne, and likewise that all of the 
said taxes for gasoline taken into the plaintiff’s airplanes at 
[fol. 8] Rock Springs for use in the said interstate com- 
merce are to be paid to the said City of Rock Springs, by 
reason whereof the said defendant, The City of Cheyenne, 
is not only claiming the sums, and each of them, that have 
heen paid by this plaintiff to the defendant, Edelman, for 
gasoline so put into its airplanes at Cheyenne for use in 
said interstate commerce, but is also claiming that the plain- 
tiff he compelled to continue such payments indefinitely for 
the future; and in like manner the said City of Rock Springs 
is not only claiming the sums, and each of them, that have 
heen paid by this plaintiff to the defendant, Edelman, for 
gasoline so put into its airplanes at Rock Springs for use 
in said interstate commerce, but it also claiming that the 
plaintiff be compelled to continue such payments indefinitely 
for the future, and by reason of the said construction of 
the said statutes and the claims so as aforesaid made by 
and on behalf of the said City of Chevenne and of the said 


8 


City of Rock Springs, each of the said municipalities is a 
necessary party to the full determination of the constitu- 
tionality of the said statutes as so as aforesaid applied to 
this plaintiff ; and the said City of Cheyenne is a necessary 
party to any full settlement and disposition of so much of 
this cause as applies to the taxes already paid by the plain- 
tiff for gasoline placed in its tanks at Cheyenne and used 
in interstate commerce as aforesaid, and the said City of 
Rock Springs is a necessary party to any full settlement 
and disposition of so much of this cause as applies to the 
taxes already paid by the plaintiff for gasoline placed in its 
tanks at Rock Springs and used in interstate commerce as 
aforesaid. 


12. And plaintiff further shows that if the exactions made 
from the plaintiff as alleged herein be held to be exactions 
of ordinary taxation upon the gasoline as property owned 
by plaintiff, in order to produce revenues for public pur- 
poses, State, County and municipal, such taxation is illegal 
and void, in that it violates the second provision of Section 
28 of Article I of the Constitution of the State of Wyoming, 
which is in these words: 


, 


‘** All taxation shall be equal and uniform.’ 


And also violates Section 11 of Article XV of the Con- 
stitution of the State of Wyoming, which is in these words, 
to-wit: 


[fol.9] ‘‘All property, except as in this constitution 
otherwise provided, shall be uniformly assessed for taxa- 
tion, and the legislature shall prescribe such regulations as 
shall secure a just valuation for taxation of all property, 
real and personal.’’ 


For that as to all other personalty within the State of 
Wyoming the owner thereof under the provisions of See- 
tion 2762 of the Wyoming Compiled Statutes of 1920, is 
taxed only upon the value of so much thereof as is owned 
by such owner on the first day of April of the then current 
year, while on the gasoline used by plaintiff as aforesaid 
the said license tax exacted from this plaintiff, as herein 
averred, is levied upon all gasoline bought or used by plain- 


LLL LLL LLL LLL PEE PLDALEL EES IE TE SIRO a 


9 


tiff during the year, although the plaintiff did not on April 
1st, or on any other day, own as much as one-tenth part of 
the gasoline upon which the said tax is exacted as in this 
bill alleged. 

And, moreover, although the highest amount of the ordi- 
nary tax for all purposes levied upon other personalty in 
any year in Cheyenne or in the County in which it is 
located, or in Rock Springs or in the County in which it is 
located or at any other place in Wyoming, does not exceed 
four per centum of the value of such personalty, yet the 
license tax levied as herein alleged against the gasoline 
used by this plaintiff in interstate commerce, as herein 
shown, exceeds twenty per centum of the value of all gaso- 
line, not only all that owned by the plaintiff on any one day 
but twenty per centum of all of that from time to time 
bought or used by the plaintiff in its said interstate com 
merce business in Wyoming. 


13. And plaintiff further alleges that each an covery the 
payments of said tax by the plaintiff has been pad solely 
in order that the plaintiff might be permitted to continue to 
use gasoline in propelling its said airplane in such inter- 
state commerce and business and in order to avou! irrepa- 
rable loss and injury by the imposition upon the plaintiff 
and its officers, agents and employees of the penalties, civil 
and criminal, imposed by said Acts, each and every of 
which penalties the said defendant, his deputies, agents, 
assistants, servants, ete., has threatened and still threatens 
to enforce and each of which the said defendant personally 
and through his said agents, deputies, officers and assistants 
will enforce unless restrained and enjoined from so doing. 


[fol.10] 14. Plaintiff further shows that under the pro- 
visions of said statutes the wholesale distributors or con- 
signees of gasoline are made agents of the State of Wyom- 
ing for the purpose of collecting said gasoline tax, said 
agents acting under the direction of the de-defendant, State 
Treasurer, and while the said distributors and/or con- 
signees of gasoline actually collect, return and remit said 
tax to the State of Wyoming and to the defendant, the 
State Treasurer, said gasoline tax is in law and in practice 
levied and imposed against the consumer of said gasoline 
within the State of Wyoming and not against the wholesale

distributor or consignee, and such tax is paid by all such 
consumers, ordinarily direct to the defendant, the Treas- 
urer of the State of Wyoming, which said tax this plaintiff 
has been forced to pay in order to purchase or consume any 
gasoline in the State of Wyoming, and will hereafter be 
forced to pay until defendant, his agents, servants, em- 
ployees, workmen and other persons acting by and through 
him, are enjoined and restrained from collecting the same 
by order of this court. 


15. And plaintiff further avers that under the laws of the 
State of Wyoming relief by injunction is a remedy afforded 
all persons as a remedy to enjoin the enforcement and/or 
collection of any illegal levy of any tax, charge or assess- 
ment and of any proceedings to enforce the same, and fur- 
ther alleges that the remedy by injunction sought by the 
plaintiff herein alone will afford to the plaintiff complete or 
speedy relief; that the plaintiff has no adequate remedy at 
law; that the entire controversy involved herein can be set- 
tled in one suit in equity in which much time and ereat ex- 
pense will be saved and a multiplicity of suits prevented, 
and that such results cannot, nor can any reasonable relief, 
be obtained by any remedy at law, either in a single suit 
brought by the plaintiff, or otherwise. 

Plaintiff further avers that under the provisions of said 
statutes the distributors of gasoline, and each of them, are 
charged with the duty as agents of the State of Wyoming of 
collecting and returning the tax to the defendant the State 
Treasurer of Wyoming, and that the plaintiff in order to 
continue to operate its said airplanes in the conduct of its 
said business is compelled to purchase from time to time, 
and from day to day, large quantities of gasoline in the 
State of Wyoming, and that no such distributor or any 
[fol.11] other dealer in gasoline in said State will sell 
such gasoline to the plaintiff unless the plaintiff so pur- 
chasing the same shall make payment of the said tax levied 
and assessed thereon at the time of the purchase thereof, 
and that hence the plaintiff is unable to purchase any gaso- 
line in the State of Wyoming for the operation or propul- 
sion of its said airplanes without at the time paying the 
said tax thereon, and plaintiff alleges that by the express 
provisions of said Acts and/or by the practical operation 
thereof by the defendant and the legal effect thereof the 


said tax is absorbed in the price paid for all gasoline pur- 
chased in Wyoming for use or consumption in the said 
State by the purchaser, including this plaintiff, and that 
under the provisions of the said Acts the said tax, although 
required to be collected and returned to the State by the 
distributor of or dealer therein as the agent of the State, 
is both in practice and in legal effect a tax on the consumer 
of such gasoline and that unless the injunctive relief herein 
sought be granted the said interstate business of the plain- 
tiff herein will be daily and constantly burdened, inter- 
ferred with and damaged, to the great and irreparable in- 
jury of the plaintiff; that this plaintiff by reason of the 
facts hereinbefore and hereinafter set forth has no clear, 
adequate or complete remedy at law, and it will suffer ir- 
reparable damage and injury and be subjected to a multi- 
plicity of vexatious suits and litigation unless the enforce- 
ment and collection of the said tax in so far as it applies to 
gasoline purchased and/or consumed by the plaintiff in the 
State of Wyoming as fuel for the operation of its said air- 
planes in interstate commerce and business as aforesaid, is 
immediately enjoined and restrained by a temporary or 
interlocutory injunction granted by this court as prayed 
for herein, and unless upon final hearing the collection and 
enforcement of the said tax be perpetually enjoined and re- 
strained as herein prayed for. 

And plaintiff avers and shows that it has paid to the 
proper officers in Wyoming all taxes justly or properly due 
from it at any place within the State of Wy oming, both on 
its gasoline and upon all its other property in Wyoming, 
including the gasoline license tax on all gasoline used by it 
locally in Wyoming.

Wherefore, plaintiff prays: 


1. That upon proper notice and hearing this court grant 
[fol.12] an interlocutory injunction restraining and en- 
joining the defendant, William H. Edelman, Treasurer of 
the State of Wyoming, his agents, servants, deputies and 
all others working under or through him as such State 
Treasurer, from the collection of said gasoline tax from 
this plaintiff during the pendency of this action. 


2. That upon final bearing of this cause it be adjudged 
and decreed by this court that the said Acts and statutes of 


12 


the Wyoming Legislature are unconstitutional, illegal and 
void as applied to this plaintiff and as applied to the said 
interstate commerce business of the plaintiff, and that the 
said defendant, William H. Edelman, as such State Treas- 
urer, and his respective deputies, agents, servants, attor- 
neys, workmen, employees and successors in office, be per- 
petually enjoined and restrained from exacting or attempt- 
ing to exact the payment of the said tax by the plaintiff 
and from collecting, or attempting to collect or enforce, 
said tax from the plaintiff on any such gasoline hereafter 
purchased and/or consumed in the State of Wyoming for 
such interstate commerce purposes, and that the defendant 
be decreed to pay the costs of this action, and that this 
court grant unto this plaintiff such other and further re- 
lief as the equity and justice of the case may require and 
which the court may deem meet and just, whether spe- 
cifically prayed for herein or not. 

John W. Lacey, Herbert V. Lacey, Attorneys for 

Plaintiff.

Separate Answer IN Equity or THE Crry or Rock Sprinas, 
A Muwnicrpant Corporation 


Filed Dee. 18, 1930 


To the Honorable, the Judge of the District Court of the 
United States, for the District of Wyoming, sitting in 
Equity: 


Comes now The City of Rock Springs, a municipal cor- 
poration, organized and existing under and by virtue 
of the laws of the State of Wyoming, and served with sub- | 
pena in the above entitled proceeding on the 27th day of 


4 
i 
3 er 


ISIGHT Sees 6 Nese RRO ITE RPL EPA PSS 


13 


November, 1930, as being one of the defendants in the above 
entitled proceeding, designated The City of Rock Springs, 
a municipal corporation, and for its separate answer to the 
bill of complaint in equity of the complainant in the above 
entitled action, admits, denies and alleges as follows: 


1. Admits that the plaintiff is a corporation existing 
under the laws of the State of Washington, and is duly au- 
thorized to do business in the State of Wyoming. 


2, Admits that the defendant, William H. Edelman, is a 
citizen and resident of the State of Wyoming, and is the 
duly elected, qualified and acting State Treasurer of the 
State of Wyoming. 


3. This defendant denies that the suit herein, or any 
cause of action stated in the plaintiff’s bill, arises under the 
Constitution and laws of the United States. 

This defendant admits that the amount in controversy 
exceeds Three Thousand Dollars ($3,000.00). 

This defendant admits that in the filing by the plaintiff 
of its bill of complaint herein, that it seeks to enjoin the 
defendant, William H. Edelman, as the State Treasurer of 
the State of Wyoming, from collecting the tax levied and 
imposed by the State of Wyoming upon the plaintiff, but 
this defendant denies that the said tax is either unconsti- 
tutional or illegal, or in violation of Clause 3 of Section 8 of 
{fol. 14] Article 1 of the Constitution of the United States, 
or otherwise, or at all. 


4. This defendant admits that the plaintiff is now engaged 
in the business of transporting passengers and express and 
United States mails by airplane for hire and profit, into, 
across and through the State of Wyoming, from points out- 
side of the boundaries thereof, and from points within the 
State of Wyoming to points outside the boundaries thereof, 
and is engaged in the similar business from points inside the 
State of Wyoming to other points within said State. 

And as to the averment that the plaintiff intends to con- 
tinue in the future to transact said business, this defendant 
has no knowledge or information sufficient to form a belief 
and therefore denies the same. 


5. This defendant admits that the fuel used in propelling 
the airplanes of the plaintiff at the present time is gasoline, 


aaa Xe = | 


14 


but as to the averment that the plaintiff cannot apportion 
and separate the amount of gasoline used upon its airplanes 
in intrastate business and commerce from interstate busi- 
ness and commerce, this defendant denies the averment. 


6. This defendant admits that the plaintiff owns, oper- 
ates and maintains airplanes in interstate commerce and in- 
trastate commerce, as averred in paragraph six of its bill 
of complaint, and that the fuel now used by said airplanes 
is gasoline, 


7. This defendant admits that a part of the gasoline used 
by the plaintiff in its said airplanes is purchased and de- 
livered to the plaintiff in the State of Wyoming. 


8. This defendant admits that some of the business and 
commerce of the plaintiff is transported in intrastate busi- 
ness, and some in interstate business, but denies that the 
said classes of business are so commingled that the separa- 
tion of one from the other is impracticable and/or uneco- 
nomical. 


9. This defendant admits that gasoline placed in the air- 
planes of the plaintiff at Rock Springs is for the months of 
March, April, May, June, July, August, September, October 
and November, as set forth in paragraph nine of the plain- 
tiff’s bill of complaint, but this defendant denies that the 
State Treasurer, his deputies, agents, servants, attorneys, 
[fol. 15] workmen, and/or employees, have wrongfully, and 
without authority of law, required the plaintiff to pay a tax 
of four cents per gallon on each and every gallon of gaso- 
line placed by the plaintiff in its airplanes while within the 
State of Wyoming. 

This defendant admits that the divers acts of the Legis- 
lature of the State of Wyoming are as set forth in said 
paragraph nine of the plaintiff’s bill of complaint. 


10. This defendant denies that the statutes of the State 
of Wyoming, and set forth in paragraph nine of the plain- 
tiff’s bill of complaint, are unconstitutional, illegal and void, 
in so far as they, or any of them, apply to the plaintiff in the 
gasoline placed by the plaintiff in its airplanes while within 
the State of Wyoming. 


11. This defendant admits that the City of Rock Springs 
is a municipal corporation organized and existing under

15 


the laws of the State of Wyoming, and that said municipal 
corporation was at all times stated in the plaintiff’s bill of 
complaint, such corporation and that the plaintiff’s air-

the airplane field of the said City of Rock Springs, and that 
all of the taxes for gasoline taken into the plaintiff’s air- 
planes at Rock Springs, are, under the law, to be paid to the 
City of Rock Springs, and that the City of Rock Springs 
is claiming for its use, all such taxes derived from said 
gasoline, so used. 


12. This defendant denies that the revenues produced 
from said gasoline taxation are in violation of the Consti- 
tution of the State of Wyoming, or at all, or that said tax 
upon gasoline is illegally assessed, or is not uniformly 
assessed.

line tax, or by the imposition of the penalties provided by 
law for the nonpayment thereof, this defendant denies the 
same,

threatened, and still threatens to enforce the penalties im-

no knowledge or information sufficient to form a belief, and 
therefore denies the same.

under the Constitution and laws of the United States; ad-

the State of Wyoming vpon plaintiff; but denies that said

and denies that the imposition of said tax is in violation of 
Clause 3 of Section 8 of Article 1 of the Constitution of the 
United States. For want of information sufficient to form

the plaintiff to transact interstate business and commerce 
in and through the State of Wyoming is far in excess of

[fol. 23] 9. The defendant, in answer to Paragraph 10 of 
Plaintiff’s Bill of Complaint, denies that the said statutes 
of Wyoming are unconstitutional, illegal and void in so far 
as they, or any or either of them, apply to plaintiff, and 
deny that the same directly burdens and interferes with 
interstate commerce, and deny that the said statutes as 
applied to plaintiff directly burdens and interferes with 
the said interstate commerce and business of plaintiff in 
transporting and carrying mail, passengers and/or express 
in interstate commerce by air; denies that the said tax is 
unconstitutional, illegal and void so far as it applies to 
plaintiff and its said business, and denies that said tax con- 
flicts with, offends against and is in violation of Clause 3 
of Section 8 of Article I of the Constitution of the United 
States granting to Congress the exclusive power to regu- 
late commerce between the several states, and denies that 
the said statutes purport to impose and levy, and denies 
that said statutes do impose and levy a tax on all gasoline 
purchased or consumed as fuel in Wyoming to propel air- 
planes flying between the said State and neighboring States 
in interstate commerce, and denies that said tax is enacted 
as the price of the privilege of using an instrumentality of 
interstate commerce and thus evading the exclusive power 
of Congress to regulate commerce between the several 
states.

11. The defendant, for its answer to Paragraph 12 of 
plaintiff’s Bill of Complaint, denies that if the exactions 
made from the plaintiff, as in said petition alleged, be 
held to be exactions of ordinary taxation upon the gaso- 
line, as property owned by plaintiff, in order to produce 
revenue for public purposes, State, County and municipal, 
that such taxation would be illegal and void as violating 
the second provision of Section 28 of Article 1 of the Con- 
stitution of the State of Wyoming, which is in the following 
words: 


‘All taxation shall be equal and uniform.”’ 


and denies that such taxation is illegal and void as violating 
Section 11 of Article 13 of the Constitution of the State of 
Wyoming, which is in the following words: 


[fol.25] ‘All property, except as in this constitution 
otherwise provided, shall be uniformly assessed for taxa- 
tion, and the legislature shall prescribe such regulations as 
shall secure a just valuation for taxation of all property, 
real and personal.’’ 


denies that said tax upon gasoline, as property owned by the 
plaintiff, is illegal and void because as to all other person- 
alty within the State of Wyoming, the owner thereof, under 
the provisions of Section 2762, Wyoming Compiled Stat- 
utes, 1920, is taxed only upon the value of so much thereof 
as is owned by such owner on the first day of April of the 
then current year, while on the gasoline used by plaintiff as 
aforesaid the said license tax exacted from this plaintiff, as 
herein averred, is levied upon all gasoline bought or used 
by plaintiff during the year, although the plaintiff did not 
on April Ist, or on any other day, own as much as one-tenth 


part of the gasoline upon which the said tax is exacted as 
in this bill alleged.

Defendant admits that the highest amount of the ordinary

year in Cheyenne or in the county in which it is located, or

tiff’s Bill of Complaint, denies that under the provisions of

15. This answering defendant, City of Cheyenne, further 
alleges that on July Ist, 1929, it entered into a written 
[fol. 28] agreement with plaintiff, a true copy of which 
agreement it attached hereto, marked Exhibit A and made 
a part hereof by this reference; that by said agreement this 
defendant leased to the plaintiff for a term of 25 years the 
four hangars and control] house, belonging to the City, lo- 
cated on said field, at an annual rental of $3,000 and also a 
rectangular tract of land, known as ‘‘hangar space,’’ 200 
feet in depth and 700 feet in width, in the vicinity of the 
buildings heretofore mentioned for the purpose of provid- 
ing space for the erection of further buildings; that the 
City also leased to said Company certain taxi-ways and 
hangar space for the sum of $1,050.00.

16. It was further agreed in said contract that the City

on behalf of defendant for not to exceed 12 years, employ-

pany, under the direction of defendant should superintend 
the maintenance, and up-keep of the field and actually per- 
form such maintenance, grading, dragging and leveling of 
the field and the repair of the lighting equipment, the City 
paying the actual cost thereof and the additional sum of 
$2,400 a year for all other services performed by plaintiff ; 
that the defendant City agreed to assist in providing police 
necessary to enforce the City regulations, governing the 
use of the field and bound itself to expenditure of large 
amounts each vear to maintain said field as one of the out- 
standing air ports of the West; that during the year 1930, 
the budget of the defendant City for its share of the ex- 
penses under said agreement was $6,650, which budget has 
proven inadequate for the many expenditures which the 
City has made in carrying out its covenants in said agree- 
mnt; that for the superintendant’s salary this defendant 
has paid $2,400; flood and beacon lights $316.03: for bound- 
ary lights $382.42; for the weather bureau $251.04; for mov- 
ing lights $562.50; for grading $1,607.50: and miscellaneous, 
an expenditure of $1,153.21; a total expenditure of $6,845.48 
up to the date of the filing of this answer; that the appro-

duly elected, qualified and acting State Treasurer of the 
State of Wyoming. 


3. This defendant, for his answer to Paragraph 3 of said 
Bill of Complaint, admits that this action is a suit of a 
civil nature; that the amount in controversy exceeds, ex- 
clusive of costs and interests, a sum in value of Three Thou- 
sand ($3,000.00) Dollars; denies that said action arises 
under the Constitution and laws of the United States; ad- 
mits that the plaintiff by this action seeks to enjoin this 
defendant, William H. Edelman, as a ‘ate officer of the 
State of Wyoming, from collecting a tax levied and imposed 
by the State of Wyoming upon plaintiff; but denies that 
said tax, so levied and imposed is unconstitutional and il- 
legal and denies that the imposition of said tax is in viola- 
tion of Clause 3 of Section 8 of Article 1 of the Constitution 
of the United States. For want of information sufficient 
to form aq belief, this defendant denies that the value of the 
right of the plaintiff to transact interstate business and 
commerce in and through the State of Wyoming is far in 
excess of Three Thousand Dollars, exclusive of interest

as aforesaid, and the said City of Rock Springs is a neces- 
sary party to any full settlement and disposition of so much 
of this cause as applies to the taxes already paid by the 
plaintiff for gasoline placed in its tanks at Rock Springs 
and used in interstate commerce as aforesaid. 


12. This defendant, for its answer to Paragraph 12 of 
[fol. 40] plaintiff’s Bill of Complaint, denies that if the ex- 
actions made from the plaintiff, as in said petition alleged, 
be held to be exactions of ordinary taxation upon the gas- 
oline, as property owned by plaintiff, in order to produce 
revenue for public purposes, State, County and municipal, 
that such taxation would be illegal and void as violating the 
second provision of Section 28 of Article 1 of the Constitu- 
tion of the State of Wyoming, which is in the following 
words: 


‘* All taxation shall be equal and uniform.”’ 
and denies that such taxation is illegal and void as violat- 


ing Section 11 of Article 15 of the Constitution of the State 
of Wyoming, which is in the following words: 


‘All property, except as in this constitution otherwise 
provided, shall be uniformly assessed for taxation, and the

4 
: 
£ 
f 


Fa FPR NES BS TRF SEN 


40 


legislature shall prescribe such regulations as shall secure 
a just valuation for taxation of all property, real and per- 
sonal.”’ 


denies that said tax upon gasoline, as property owned by 
the plaintiff, is illegal and void because as to all other per- 
sonalty within the State of Wyoming, the owner thereof, 
under the provisions of Section 2762, Wyoming Compiled 
Statutes, 1920, is taxed only upon the value of so much 
thereof as is owned by such owner on the first day of April 
of the then current year, while on the gasoline used by 
plaintiff as aforesaid the said license tax exacted from this 
plaintiff, as herein averred, is levied upon all gasoline 
bought or used by plaintiff during the year, although the 
plaintiff did not on April Ist, or on any other day, own as 
much as one-tenth part of the gasoline upon which the said 
tax is exacted as in this bill alleged. 

Defendant admits that the highest amount of the ordi- 
nary tax for all purposes levied upon other personalty in 
any year in Cheyenne or in the county in which it is located, 
or in Rock Springs or in the county in which it is located 
or at any other place in Wyoming, does not exceed four 
per centum of the value of such personalty, yet the license 
tax levied as herein alleged against the gasoline used by 
this plaintiff in interstate commerce, as herein shown, ex- 
ceeds twenty per centum of the value of all gasoline, not 
only all that owned by the plaintiff on any one day but 
twenty per centum of all of that from time to time bought 
or used by the plaintiff in its said interstate commerce busi- 
{fol. 41] ness in Wyoming; but denies that said license tax 
exacted from said plaintiff is illegal and void by reason 
thereof. 


13. This defendant, for its answer to Paragraph 13 of 
Plaintiff’s Bill of Complaint, denies that each and every of 
the payments of said tax by the plaintiff has been paid 
solely in order that the plaintiff might be permitted to 
continue to use gasoline in propelling its said artplanes in 
such interstate commerce and business and in order to 
avoid irreparable loss and injury by the imposition upon 
the plaintiff and its officers, agents and employees of the 
penalties, civil and criminal, imposed by said acts, each 
and every of which penalties the said defendant, his depu- 


41 


ties, agents, assistants. servants, ete., has threatened and 
still threatens to enforce and each of which the said de- 
fendant personally and through his said agents, deputies, 
officers and assistants will enforce unless restrained and 
enjoined from so doing. 


14. This defendant, for answer to Paragraph 14 of plain- 
tiff’s Bill of Complaint, denies that under the provisions 
of said statutes the wholesale distributors or consignees 
of gasoline are made agents of the State of Wyoming for 
the purpose of collecting said gasoline tax, said agents 
acting under the direction of the defendant, State Treas- 
urer, and while the said distributors and/or consignees 
of gasoline actually collect, return and remit said tax to 
the State of Wyoming and to the defendant, the State 
Treasurer, and denies that said gasoline tax is in law and 
in practice levied and imposed against the consumer of 
said gasoline within the State of Wyoming and not against 
the wholesale distributor or consignee, and such tax is paid 
by all such consumers, ordinarily direct to the defendant, 
the Treasurer of the State of Wyoming; and admits that 
said plaintiff has paid tax in order to purchase or con- 
sume any gasoline in the State of Wyoming, and will here- 
after be forced to pay until defendant, his agents, serv- 
ants, employees, workmen and other persons acting by and 
through him, are enjoined and restrained from collecting 
the same by order of this court. 


15. This defendant, for its answer to Paragraph 15 of 
Plaintiff’s Bill of Complaint, admits that under the laws 
of the State of Wyoming relief by injunction is a remedy 
afforded all persons as a remedy to enjoin the enforcement 
and/or collection of any illegal levy of any tax, charge or 
[fol. 42] assessment and of any proceedings to enforce the 
same, and further alleges that the remedy by injunction 
sought by the plaintiff herein alone will afford to the plain- 
tiff complete or speedy relief; that the plaintiff has no ade- 
quate remedy at law; that the entire controversy involved 
herein can be settled in one suit in equity in which much 
time and great expense will be saved and a multiplicity of 
suits prevented, and that such results cannot, nor can any 
reasonable relief, be obtained by any remedy at law, either 
in a single suit brought by the plaintiff, or otherwise.

This defendant denies that under the provisions of said

Wyoming, of collecting and returning the tax to defend-

the State of Wyoming and that no such distributor or any 
other dealer in gasoline in said state will sell such gasoline 
to the plaintiff unless the plaintiff so purchasing the same 
shall make payment of said tax levied and assessed thereon 
at the time of the purchase thereof; and admits that plain-

state by the distributor of or dealer therein is both in

gasoline; and denies that unless the injunctive relief herein

airplanes in interstate commerce and business, as afore- 
said, is tmmediately enjoined and restrained by a tempo- 
rary interlocutory injunction granted by this court, as 
prayed for in plaintiff’s petition, and unless defendant be 
upon final hearing of said cause perpetually enjoined and 
restrained from the collection and enforcement of said tax.

that the plaintiff may file a supplemental Bill of Complaint 
in said cause, setting out payments of the gasoline tax com- 
plained of, made to Harry R. Weston, State Treasurer,

nal Bill and not by the statement at large such facts as the 
plaintiff may desire to allege against the said Weston. 

It is further stipulated and agreed that the answer here- 
tofore filed in said cause by the defendant Edelman may 
stand and be taken also as an answer by the defendant 
Weston.

[fol. 47] In Untrep States District Court 


Findings of Fact, Conclusions of Law and Decree—Filed 
July 23, 1931 


The above entitled cause was heretofore heard by the 
Court upon the pleadings, the evidence and the arguments 
of counsel and taken under advisement by the court. 

And the court having heard and considered the said 
pleadings and evidence and the arguments of counsel, and 
being now sufficiently advised, does upon the matters in- 
volved make the following findings of fact, conclusions of 
law and decree. 


I 


FINDINGS or Facr 


1. The court finds that no application was made by any 
party to this cause for any hearing upon the prayer for an 
interlocutory injunction, and further finds that the case pro- 
ceeded to final hearing before a single judge, without objec- 
tion on the part of any party to the cause, and that the 
plaintiff waived its application for an interlocutory injune- 
tion as contained in its bill of complaint, which waiver was 
consented to and acquiesced in by all of the defendants. 

And the court finds that the plaintiff is a corporation 
organized and existing under the laws of the State of 
Washington and duly authorized to do business of the kind 
mentioned in the Bill of Complaint, in and through the 
State of Wyoming, and is a citizen and resident of said 
State of Washington. 


2. That the defendants William H. Edelman and Harry 
R. Weston are citizens and residents of the State of Wyo- 
ming, and that at the time of the filing of the Bill of Com- 
plaint the defendant William H. Edelman was an officer of 
the State of Wyoming, namely, the duly elected, qualified 
and acting State Treasurer of the State of Wyoming, and 
that later, to-wit, on the 5th day of January, 1931, the said 
defendant Harry R. Weston, having theretofore been duly 
elected, became the qualified and acting State Treasurer of 
the State of Wyoming. 

3. That the defendant, The City of Cheyenne, and the de- 
fendant, The City of Rock Springs, and each of them, are 
municipal corporations, organized and existing under the 


Di tele 


ee ea 


a 2 


47 


laws of the State of Wyoming, and each of them is a citizen 
[fol. 48] and resident of the State of Wyoming; that at 
Cheyenne there is a municipal air field owned by Cheyenne, 
at which the airplanes used by the plaintiff land more or 
less regularly, and more or less regularly are supplied with 
gasoline to be used as fuel in propelling its airplanes in its 
interstate commerce; that at Rock Springs there is a mu- 
nicipal air field leased by said last named City from the 
United States of America at a rental of $10.00 per annum, 
at which the airplanes used by the plaintiff land more or 
less regularly, and more or less regularly are supplied with 
gasoline to be used as fuel in propelling its airplanes in its 
interstate commerce. 

That the State of Wyoming, through its State Treas- 
urers, is insisting upon payment of all the taxes in con- 
troversy in this cause, and the said City of Cheyenne is 
claiming the taxes paid by the plaintiff for gasoline put 
into plaintiff’s airplanes at Cheyenne for use in its inter- 
state commerce, and is insisting that the collection of the 
same for the use of said City be continued. Likewise the 
City of Rock Springs is claiming the taxes paid by the 
plaintiff for gasoline put into plaintiff’s airplanes at Rock 
Springs for use in its interstate commerce, and is insisting 
that the collection of the same for the use of said City be 
continued. And the said City of Cheyenne and the said 
City of Rock Springs are proper and necessary parties to 
this action. 

4. That the plaintiff’s suit exhibited by its Bill of Com- 
plaint and by its evidence is a suit of a civil nature and 
arises under the Constitution and laws of the United States, 
and the matter in controversy exceeds, exclusive of interest 
and costs, the sum and value of Three Thousand Dollars. 

That the action is one in which the plaintiff seeks to en- 
join the defendant William H. Edelman and the defendant 
Harry R. Weston, State officers of the State of Wyoming 
as above found, from collecting a tax which plaintiff claims 
is an unconstitutional and illegal tax levied and imposed 
by the State of Wyoming upon the plaintiff in violation of 
Clause 3 of Section 8 of Article 1 of the Constitution of the 
United States, and that the value of the alleged right of 
the plaintiff to transact interstate business and commerce 
in and through the State of Wyoming without being sub- 
jected to such tax is in excess of Three Thousand Dollars 


48 


exclusive of interest and costs; that unless the defendants 
be restrained from the collection of such tax, plaintiff 
[fol. 49] would be forced to pay to the defendants William 
EK. Edelman and his successor, Harry R. Weston, such taxes 
at each of said fields in an amount in excess of Three Thou- 
sand Dollars exclusive of interest and costs, in order to 
carry on its interstate business and commerce as herein- 
after found. 


5. That the plaintiff is now and for a long time hitherto 
has been engaged in, and intends to continue to carry on 
and transact interstate commerce and business in transport- 
ing passengers, express and United States mail by airplane, 
for hire and profit, into, across and through the State of 
Wyoming from points outside the boundaries thereof, and 
from points within the State of Wyoming to points outside 
the boundaries thereof, with a small amount of similar 
business, hereinafter referred to, from points inside said 
State of Wyoming to other points within said State. 


6. That the fuel used in propelling the plaintiff’s air- 
planes is gasoline exclusively; that the substantial portion 
of plaintiff's business is interstate; that the Wyoming in- 
trastate transportation of mails, passengers and express is 
an insignificant percentage of its entire business, so that 
it would be impractical and impossisble to determine or 
estimate the relative amount of gasoline which would be 
consumed in carrying on its intrastate business. 


7. That the plaintiff owns, operates and maintains air- 
planes in said interstate commerce and business upon regu- 
larly defined routes and schedules, the motive power used 
by its airplanes, and each of them, being exclusively the 
use of gasoline as fuel, as aforesaid. 


8. That part of such gasoline used by the plaintiff is 
purchased by and delivered to plaintiff in the State of 
Wyoming; that a part thereof is purchased outside of said 
State, and all thereof as purchased is deposited in tanks at 
said municipal airports in Wyoming, and that such gasoline 
as is used and consumed by plaintiff as fuel for the propul- 
sion of its said airplanes in its said interstate commerce 
and business is an instrumentality of interstate commerce. 
To which finding the defendants, and each of them, now 
object and except.

9. That the said interstate business and commerce and 
the said intrastate business and commerce are carried in 
[fol. 50] the same planes, and that the said intrastate busi- 
ness could not be pratically or economically carried in sepa- 
rate planes. 


10. That the plaintiff offers for sale and sells gasoline at 
retail prices to customers desiring to purchase the same at 
either of the municipal landing fields here involved and 
collects from such customers so purchasing gasoline the 
retail price charged therefor, including the amount of the 
State tax levied under the statute of the State of Wyoming.

cents per gallon on all gasoline sold by it in Wyoming and 
on all gasoline used by it in Wyoming other than that used 
in its said interstate commerce, and the said taxes so paid 
without protest have been paid over to the said City of 
Cheyenne and the said City of Rock Springs in accordance 
with their respective rights therein.

The plaintiff has likewise paid all ordinary taxes that 
have accrued on all its property within the State of Wyo-

11. That on the 1st day of July, A. D. 1929, the said City 
of Cheyenne and the plaintiff herein entered into a contract 
in writing concerning the Cheyenne airport and landing 
field, which provided, among other things, 


(a) That the said City let to the plaintiff for the term of 
twenty-five years from the date of said contract four 
hangars and a control house at said airport, described in 
said contract, and the plaintiff agreed to pay for the use of 
said buildings the sum of $3,000.00 per year, and to keep 
said buildings insured at its own expense. 


(b) That said City let to the plaintiff for the term of 
twenty-five years from the date of said contract, a certain 
rectangular tract of land described in said contract as 
hangar space, and the plaintiff agreed to erect thereon a 
building to cost not less than $50,000.00, in connection with 
which the City also let to the plaintiff certain described faxi- 
ways in connection with the said buildings and the said 
hangar space, and the plaintiff agreed to maintain, repair, 
| fol. 52] and, if necessary for its use, improve the said taxi- 


51 


ways, and at its own expense furnish such light thereon as 
might be necessary for its own use. 

And the plaintiff further agreed to pay to the said City 
an annual rental of $1,050.00 for said hangar space and taxi- 
ways. 


(c) In and by said contract the plaintiff and the said City 
of Cheyenne agreed that all of the space at said municipal 
air field, after excluding therefrom that portion covered by 
the buildings, hangar-space and taxi-ways aforesaid, should 
constitute a landing field, and the City agreed to keep the 
said landing field during the term of said agreement and 
during the entire period in which the plaintiff might occupy 
any portion of said municipal field as a tenant of said City, 
as a suitable, well-maintained and well regulated airplane 
landing field for the reasonable use of any and all planes 
and/or airships operated by the plaintiff. And the said 
City further agreed that the plaintiff should have rights on 
said landing field and on the municipal field equal to those 
granted to any other user. 


(d) In and by said contract it was further agreed that the 
said landing field as it had theretofore existed should be 
enlarged by adding a new area already owned by the City, 
and that the plaintiff and the said City should proceed to 
grade and prepare said new area to be included, and extend 
the lighting system around the same, and agreed that the 
cost of all the work of preparing and grading the said new 
area and the cost of the new lighting equipment and the in- 
stallation thereof should be divided equally between the 
parties, the total cost not to exceed $7,000.00. 


(e) In and by said contract the City further agreed to use 
all reasonable efforts to acquire, at its own expense, the land 
included within an additional area described in said con- 
tract, ar 1 after acquiring the same and during the entire 
period in which the plaintiff might make any use of said 
municipal field under the provisions of said contract, agreed 
that it would keep all of said new area, or as much thereof as 
it might acquire free from all buildings and obstructions. 
And the said defendant, the City of Cheyenne, by the said 
contract obligated itself to provide and maintain its said air- 
port landing field and permit the plaintiff unlimited, but not

oressenn ORAM NTE

exclusive use thereof in connection with its business; and the 
[fol.53] defendant, the City of Cheyenne, has expended 
substantial sums of money in improving said airport land- 
ing field and maintaining the same, and will be compelled 
to spend additional substantial sums for the same purpose, 
and that the plaintiff is the chief user of said field at Chey- 
enne. 


(f) And in and by said contract the City of Cheyenne 
further agreed that the plaintiff should be entitled to the 
full, but not exclusive use of said municipal field and land- 
ing field during the term of said agreement on terms as 
favorable as might be granted by the City to any other 
used thereof, and that no charge for any use which it might 
make thereof should be exacted from the plaintiff additional 
to that in said contract specifically provided. 


(g) And that the plaintiff should receive in full for all 
services performed by it in superintending the municipal 
field and landing field and in directing traffic thereon and 
in providing supervising officers and caretakers and in full 
for all office expense and time of officers and accounting, 
and in full for operating and lighting equipment, and in 
full for directing the maintenance and upkeep of the munici- 
pal field, the sum of twenty-four hundred dollars ($2,- 
400.00) per year. 


It was further agreed in said contract that the plaintiff 
should, under the City’s direction, perform all of the actual 
work of maintaining the municipal field, including the grad- 
ing, dragging, conditioning and leveling of the landing field 
and the maintenance and repair of the lighting equipment, 
and that the City should pay to the plaintiff the actual cost 
thereof to the plaintiff, excluding all charges for services 
performed by the plaintiff in superintendence, ete., the pay- 
ment for such superintendence, ete., being $2,400.00 per an- 
num, as above stated. 


12. On the 21st day of July, 1930, the said City of Rock 
Springs and the plaintiff herein entered into a contract in 
writing, which provided, among other things, 

(a) That the City let to the plaintiff for the period of 
twenty vears the non-exclusive use of its municipal airport 
and landing field, the plaintiff to pay to the said City

$600.00 per year for use of the said air field and of a parcel 
of land 300 feet by 150 feet on which the plaintiff was to

[fol. 54] thereon insured for and on behalf of the City at

on said field; to keep all such lighting equipment in good,

to supervise, manage and maintain said airport and air 
field and the improvements and equipment thereon without 
expense to the City. é' 


And it was further agreed in the contract with each of 
said cities that the City should impose no charges of any 
kind or character upon materials and/or products fur- 
nished or otherwise acquired by the plaintiff for use on said 
airport, nor should any charge be imposed by said City for 
materials and/or products prior to their acquisition by the 
said lessee, 

By said contract the said City of Rock Springs further 
agreed that it should bear all expense incident to the main- 
tenance and upkeep of said airport and the improvements 
and equipment thereon except as otherwise in said con- 
tract specifically provided. 

Said contract also contains the following specific pro- 
visions: 


‘*The lessee (the plaintiff here) agrees to supervise and 
manage said landing field for and on behalf of the lessor 
and under the direction of said lessor. The lessee further 
agrees to supervise and manage on behalf of the lessor and 
under the direction of the lessor the maintenance and up- 
keep of said landing field and the improvements and equip- 
ment thereof; said supervision and management to be 
without cost to the said lessor. 

‘Tt is agreed that the lessee may at its option and after 
giving the lessor 90 days notice, terminate its obligation 
and duty to supervise and manage said landing field and 
airport and the maintenance and upkeep thereof. It is 
agreed that if the lessee shall terminate its obligation and 
duty to supervise and manage said landing field and air-

a! 


54 


port, as aforesaid, that the annual rental to be paid by the 
lessee hereunder shall be increased not more than Thirty- 
six Hundred Dollars ($3,600.00), during entire term of 
lease.’’ 


13. And the court finds that the charge for gasoline taxes 
[fol. 55] against the plaintiff for gasoline placed in its air- 
planes at Cheyenne and used in interstate commerce as 
aforesaid, was not in any wise, nor was any part thereof 
exacted from the plaintiff in or by the said contract be- 
tween the plaintiff and the City of Cheyenne, nor were the 
same or any part thereof chargeable against the plaintiff 
at the time when said contract was made with the City of 
Cheyenne. To which finding the defendants, and each of 
them, now object and except. 


14. On December 21, 1929, the Legislature of the State of 
Wyoming enacted the statute which (if the same be valid 
against the plaintiff in relation to the matters here in- 
volved) for the first time required the payment of such 
taxes on the gasoline placed in plaintiff’s airplanes at Chey- 
enne and used by plaintiff as aforesaid in interstate com- 
merce, which statute, if valid as against plaintiff as to said 
gasoline used in interstate commerce, made such taxes col- 
lectible for the use and benefit of the defendant, the City of 
Cheyenne. 

And the court finds that the said plaintiff has in all re- 
spects performed its said contracts, and each of them, and 
that the same, and each of them, are in full force. To 
which finding the defendants, and each of them, now object 
and except.

16. And the court finds that during the period when these 
taxes were so as aforesaid collected the highest amount of 
ordinary tax for all purposes levied upon other personalty 
[fol. 57] in any year in Cheyenne, or in the County in which 
it is located, or in Rock Springs, or in the county in which 
it is located, or at any other place in Wyoming, did not ex- 
ceed four per centum of the value of such personalty as was 
owned on April Ist of the year of such levy by the person 
against whom the tax was levied, but the license tax against 
the gasoline used by the plaintiff in interstate commerce as 
above found exceeds twenty per centum of the value of all 
gasoline, not only all that owned by the plaintiff on any one 
day but twenty per centum of all that from time to time 
bought or used by the plaintiff in said interstate commerce 
business in Wyoming, although the plaintiff did not on 
April first of any year, or on any other day, own as much 
as one-tenth part of the gasoline upon which the said tax 
was exacted. 

That the said State of Wyoming by virtue of the provi- 
sions of Chapter 73, Session Laws of Wyoming, 1923; 
Chapter 89, Session Laws of Wyoming, 1925; Chapter 70, 
Session Laws of Wyoming, 1927; Chapter 139, Session 
Laws of Wyoming, 1929; Chapter 14, Session Laws of 
Wyoming, 1929, Special Session, to the extent that the same 
are valid concerning the gasoline used in interstate com- 
merce, as herein found, through the State Treasurers of the 
State of Wyoming imposes and collects a tax per gallon 
upon all gasoline used by plaintiff at the municipal air field 
at Rock Springs and on all that used by plaintiff at the 
municipal air field at Cheyenne, Wyoming, including the 
gasoline placed by plaintiff in its airplanes at said fields 
used in both interstate and intrastate business. 

That the plaintiff has segregated that portion of the gaso- 
line used by it in propelling its airships in interstate com- 
merce from the gasoline used and/or sold by it for other 
purposes at said municipal air fields. 


—ooOoorr,— rh 


57 


17. The protested taxes here involved are still retained 
by the Treasurers to whom paid, that is to say, those paid 
to the defendant Edelman are still retained by him, and 
those paid to the defendant Weston are still retained by 
him. 

The non-protested taxes on gasoline, being taxes on gaso- 
line used otherwise than in interstate commerce, have been 
paid to the said cities respectively, the amount of such non- 
protested taxes upon gasoline used at the municipal air- 
port at Cheyenne being by the Treasurer paid over to the 
[fol. 58] said City of Cheyenne, and the amount of such 
non-protested taxes upon gasoline used at the municipal 
airport at Rock Springs being by the Treasurer paid over 
to the said City of Rock Springs. The amount of sueh 
money so received by the City of Cheyenne is retained in a 
separate fund for the use of the said air field. During the 
years last past not all of the money so received and so re- 
tained in this separate fund by the City of Cheyenne has 
been expended, but all of the same that was actually ex- 
pended was expended in connection with its said airport. 
The amount of such non-protested taxes received by the 
City of Cheyenne when added to the rentals received by 
said City from the plaintiff under the aforesaid contract 
exceeded the expense to said City of the maintenance of 
said air field and its supervision, during the specific time 
covered by the evidence in this cause. 

The municipal airport at the City of Rock Springs was 
maintained and supervised at the sole expense of the plain- 
tiff and without expense to said City of Rock Springs, and 
the plaintiff in addition has paid to the said City of Rock 
Springs for the use of said airport at the rate of $600.00 
per annum. During the specific time covered by the evi- 
dence in this cause the amount of such non-protested taxes 
received by the City of Rock Springs, when added to the 
rentals received by the said City from the plaintiff under 
its aforesaid contract, exceeded the expense to said City of 
the maintenance of said air field and its supervision. 


18. At the recent popular election in Cheyenne a bond 
issue was voted upon and approved by the people, authoriz- 
ing the expenditure of $15,000.00 for various purposes in 
connection with the improvement of its municipal air field. 


58 


19. That the gasoline upon which the tax was paid under 
protest was held in storage tanks owned by the plaintiff at 
said municipal airports, and the gasoline thus stored was at 
all times subject to such use as plaintiff might desire to 
make thereof. 


20. That the plaintiff, in the operation of its air trans- 
port line operates upon regular schedule between Chicago, 
Illinois, and San Francisco, California, and enjoys in con- 
nection with such operations a contract with the United 
States Government for the transportation of United States 
mail and also transports in its airplanes upon such regular 
[fol. 59] schedule between said designated points and inter- 
mediate points, passengers and express for hire. 


21. That in the operation of its airlines, it makes use of 
the municipal air landing fields located at Cheyenne, Wyo- 
ming, and at Rock Springs, Wyoming, at which points its 
planes are landed on schedule for discharge and reception 
of mail, passengers and express and to be refueled, ex- 
amined and conditioned. 


22. That the defendants, City of Cheyenne and City of 
Rock Springs, have each entered into agreements with the 
plaintiff whereby they have obligated themselves to provide 
and maintain said airport landing fields and to permit the 
plaintiff unlimited but not exclusive use thereof in connec- 
tion with its business. 


23. That said tax of four cents per gallon on gasoline 
imposed upon the plaintiff, is likewise imposed upon other- 
similarly situated, with the uniform application. To which 
finding, and to each separate matter therein, the plaintiff 
now at the time when the same is made excepts. 


24. That the plaintiff’s gasoline upon which such tax is 
imposed, has not previously been subjected to such tax, 
either within or without the State of Wyoming. To which 
finding, and to each separate matter therein, the plaintiff 
now at the time when the same is made excepts.

Conciusions or Law 


And upon the facets found as aforesaid the court makes 
the following conclusions of law, to-wit:

2 


1. That the statute of December 21, 1929, so far as the 
same levies, or attempts to levy a tax of four cents per ~ 
gallon on gasoline taken into plaintiff’s airplanes at _ 
Cheyenne, and used as an instrumentality of interstate — 
commerce in propelling plaintiff’s airplanes in such com- ~ 
merce, was and is constitutional and valid under the facts 
and circumstances above found. To which finding and con- 
clusion of law the plaintiff now at the time excepts. 

And the court finds that the statute of December 21, 1929, 
so far as the same levies, or attempts to levy a tax of four 
cents per gallon on gasoline taken into plaintiff’s airplanes 
[fol. 60] at Rock Springs, and used as an instrumentality ; 
of intersiate commerce in propelling plaintiff’s airplanes 
in such commerce, was and is constitutional and valid under 
the facts and circumstances above found. To which find- 
ing and conclusion of law the plaintiff now at the time 
excepts. 


oh SERRE 


2. (a) That this action by the plaintiff so far as relates 
to the collection and threatened collection under the Wyo- 
ming Statute of December 21, 1929, of the tax of four 
cents per gallon on gasoline taken into plaintiff’s airplanes 
at Cheyenne and used in propelling said airplanes in plain- 
tiff’s interstate commerce, should be dismissed on the 
merits. To which finding and conclusion of law the plain- 
tiff now at the time excepts. 


(b) That this action by the plaintiff so far as relates to 
the collection and threatened collection under the Wyoming 
Statute of December 21, 1929, of the tax of four cents per 
gallon on gasoline taken into plaintiff’s airplanes at Rock 
Springs and used in propelling said airplanes in plaintiff’s 
interstate commerce, should be dismissed on the merits. 
To which finding and conclusion of law the plaintiff now 
at the time excepts, 


Ill 
DECREE 


It is therefore considered, ordered and adjudged by the 
court that as to the taxes on gasoline complained of taken 
into plaintiff’s airplanes at Cheyenne as an instrumental- 
ity of interstate commerce, the injunction prayed for is 


‘ : ' . . . = ™ ~ooteey Leen 


ee as 


& 


LEAR SOR Git s 


Bs 


60 


denied, and this cause is dismissed on the merits at the 
cost of the plaintiff. To which decree and order of the 
court, and to each matter therein, the plaintiff now at the 
time excepts. 

It is further considered, ordered and adjudged by the 
court that as to the taxes on gasoline complained of taken 
into plaintiff’s airplanes at Rock Springs as an instrumen- 
tality of interstate commerce, the injunction prayed for is 
denied, and this cause is dismissed on the merits at the 
costs of the plaintiff. To which decree and order of the 
court, and to each matter therein, the plaintiff now at the 
time excepts. . 

It is further ordered and decreed that the defendants, 
and each of them, do go hence without day and do have and 
recover of and from the plaintiff their costs herein, to be 
[fol.61] taxed. To which order and decree and to each 
part thereof the plaintiff now at the time excepts. 

And the plaintiff now prays an appeal herein to the 
United States Circuit Court for the Tenth Judicial Circuit, 
which prayer is now granted by the court. 

And the court now fixes the sum of $500.00 as the amount 
in which the plaintiff shall file its undertaking and bond, 
with proper surety, to be approved by this court, for the 
payment of costs on said appeal. 

Dated this 23d day of July, A. D. 1931. 

T. Blake Kennedy, Judge.

EVIDENCE 


For the purpose of this trial the defendants admit that 
the plaintiff is a corporation authorized to do business in 
Wyoming, as alleged in the petition. 


Testimony of Huan CorrmMan, a witness called on behalf 
of plaintiff: 


Direct examination: 


IT am Transfer Clerk on behalf of the United States Gov- 
ernment at the air field at Cheyenne and know what mail is 
handled by plaintiff through that field. The mail delivered 
at Rock Springs and taken up at Rock Springs for de- 
[fol. 62] livery elsewhere runs from one letter to twelve by 
each plane. Twelve would be a very large amount. Noth- 
ing originates at Rock Springs for Cheyenne. Practically 
all from Cheyenne to Rock Springs comes from outside of 
Wyoming and is interstate. The Rock Springs mail comes 
from Chicago, Omaha, Lincoln and North Platte, and I 
put it into a small pouch for Rock Springs. The pouch 
with its contents averages a pound and four ounces in 
weight. The pouch with its lock weighs thirteen ounces. 
The mail for Rock Springs and that for Los Angeles are 
put in the same compartment. 


Cross-examination: 


The Rock Springs mail is in the same mail pit with other 
mail going to other points but is in a separate pouch. I 
deliver the mail to the employee of the Boeing Company. 
No other mail goes to Rock Springs except that in the Rock 
Springs pouch. I know nothing about the express going to 
Rock Springs from other points. No mail goes east from 
Rock Springs. The only mail to Rock Springs is sent from 
Cheyenne. None is from other points in the State. It is 
from Cheyenne and from points south and east. There is 
no mail pouch from Rock Springs to Cheyenne. The mail 
from Rock Springs is never handled by the Boeing Com- 
pany until it reaches Cheyenne. It comes to Cheyenne by 


BOP A dy NEE TS ieddeiateaeaii, 


> PRIS FOES RI tet 


. 


62 


eastbound trains and is sent to the field at Cheyenne. No 
mail from Rock Springs addressed to points east is received 
at Rock Springs by the Boeing Company and carried to 
Salt Lake for transfer. Trains coming from the north and 
from Denver to Cheyenne and all trains centering at Chey- 
enne send all of their mail to the air field, and this pouch 
with Rock Springs mail as made up never exceeds twelve 
letters. Practically all of that comes from the east and 
south interstate. The Boeing planes going east do not stop 
at Rock Springs and carry no mail east from Rock Springs.

Exuisir A 


‘*‘Now, Therefore, pursuant to the authority in me vested 
by the provisions of said Act, I hereby certify that said 
Boeing Air Transport, Incorporated, of Seattle, Washing- 
ton, shall have the right, so long as it complies with all 
rules, regulations, and orders that may be issued by the 
Postmaster General for meeting the needs of the Postal 
Service and adjusting mail operation to advances in the 
art of flying and passenger transportation, to carry air 
mail over the route hereinafter set out or any modification 
thereof, at rates of compensation fixed herein, or to be 
fixed from time to time, at least annually, by the Postmas- 
ter General, provided that such rates shall not exce-d $1.25 
per mile for a period ending not more than ten years from 
the date of beginning service under said contract, to-wit, 
on the Fifth day of April, 1936; subject to the following 
conditions: 


Pe 


DUSTED ADMIT LOM AEN 


Terms and Conditions 


‘*1, The issuance of this certificate by the Postmaster 
General and its acceptance by the Carrier as hereinafter 
provided shall be deemed a full mutual release of the United 
States and the said Carrier from all the terms, conditions, 
and obligations of the air mail contract hereinbefore re- 
ferred to, dated the twenty-ninth day of January, 1927, from 
the date of the acceptance of this certificate, except com- 
pensation due for services performed prior to the date 
hereof, and upon the acceptance thereof the said Carrier 
shall surrender the said contract to the United States. 


‘*2. The route over which the Carrier shall have the right 
to carry air mail shall be from Chicago, Illinois, by Certain 
Designated Points, to San Francisco, California, and Re- 
turn, including any extension or other modification of said 
route that may be made as hereinafter provided. 


**3. Upon 60 days’ notice to the Carrier, the Posimaster 
General from time to time may modify said route by an ex- 
tension or extensions thereof, including lateral extensions 
or consolidations, and prescribe the schedule therefor in- 
| fol. 64] cluding the stops thereon, and determine the mile- 
age upon which the compensation of the Carrier is to be 
based, 


‘*4. For the purposes of this certificate the distance be- 
tween the terminal points of the route hereinbefore pre- 
scribed shall be deemed to be 2,025 miles. 


**5. The aireraft employed by the Carrier in the service 
authorized hereby shall be manufactured in the United 
States. They shall be of modern design, airworthy, and 
suitable for the service prescribed; the design and capacity 
of such aircraft, and the location, size and construction of 
the mail compartments therein, shall be subject to the ap- 
proval of the Postmaster General. 

‘*The following service may be prescribed by the Post- 
master General:

‘**7. Upon 60 days notice to the Carrier, the Postmaster 
General may increase, diminish, or modify the service above 
prescribed, and make such adjustments in the compensation 
of the Carrier as he may deem proper. 


**8. The Carrier shall transport the mails of the United 
States offered for carriage over the route hereinbefore set 
out with due celerity, in a safe and secure manner, free from 
wet or other injury, receiving and delivering the same at 
fields and points therein designated on schedule prescribed 
by the Postmaster General. 


‘*9. Payments shall be made to the Carrier monthly upon 
evidence that the service authorized hereby has been well 
and faithfully performed in accordance with the provisions 
hereof. 


‘10. The Carrier shall keep and maintain an accurate 
system of accounting in accordance with regulations pre- 


o—9d71
